T<sub>arge</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>can</sub> <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e</sub> <sub>wor</sub>kfl<sub>ow</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>an</sub>d the policy parameters. $$

H<sub>ere,</sub> $\kappa _ { j }$ identifies the context variant that generated trajec-<sup>tor</sup>y $\tau _ { j } ,$ <sub>an</sub>d $z _ { j }$ <sub>recor</sub>d<sub>s</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>rese</sub>t id<sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y.</sub> O<sub>n</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>presen</sub>t b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> $D _ { t }$ <sub>can</sub> <sub>rece</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> <sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> their rewards. Reward-guided context update. At each round, it sam<sub>p</sub>les alread<sub>y</sub> mate-<sub>r</sub>i<sub>a</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>s, eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>em on ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>rese</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>see</sub>d<sub>s, an</sub>d <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r ra</sub>ti<sub>ngs us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>re-</sub> <sub>war</sub>d<sub>s.</sub> A f<sub>rozen re</sub>fl<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> th<sub>en con</sub>t<sub>ras</sub>t<sub>s success</sub>f<sub>u</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>races</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>proposes</sub> <sub>a</sub> t<sub>ype</sub>d <sub>wor</sub>k<sub>-</sub> fl<sub>ow rev</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o a</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>-ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>paren</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>c</sub>hild <sub>en</sub>t<sub>ers</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>popu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er</sub> <sub>pass</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>sc</sub>h<sub>ema, provenance, an</sub>d li<sub>n</sub>t <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>s.</sub> B<sub>ecause</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>hild i<sub>s</sub> <sub>propose</sub>d <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e curren</sub>t <sub>roun</sub>d’<sub>s ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> it d<sub>oes</sub> <sub>no</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>h<sub>er</sub>it th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>rewar</sub>d<sub>s.</sub> It<sub>s</sub> <sub>u</sub>tilit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> it i<sub>s</sub> <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>sequen</sub>t <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> At th<sub>e en</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>es</sub>t<sub>-ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>wor</sub>kfl<sub>ow</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>oymen</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Task–context-matched policy update. The policy update learns online from trajectories generated by the current poli<sub>cy ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> f<sub>rom a</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>.</sub> B<sub>ecause</sub> th<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t condition changes the efective policy input, trajectories are <sub>par</sub>titi<sub>one</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o groups s</sub>h<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>an</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>:</sub>

$$
G (q, \kappa) = \left\{j \mid q _ {j} = q, \kappa_ {j} = \kappa \right\}.
$$

For a group G of size $n _ { G }$ , w<sup>e</sup> <sup>com</sup>pu<sup>te</sup>

$$
\bar {r} _ {G} = \frac {1}{n _ {G}} \sum_ {j \in G} r _ {j}, \qquad A _ {j} = \frac {r _ {j} - \bar {r} _ {G}}{s _ {G}},
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $s _ { G }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e norma</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>or:</sub> it i<sub>s se</sub>t t<sub>o one</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>mean-cen</sub>t<sub>ere</sub>d <sub>a</sub>d<sub>van</sub>t<sub>ages,</sub> <sub>or</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n-group</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d d<sub>ev</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on p</sub>l<sub>us a sma</sub>ll <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>an</sub>t f<sub>or s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>di<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>a</sub>d<sub>van</sub>t<sub>ages.</sub> Si<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>an</sub>t<sub>-rewar</sub>d <sub>groups prov</sub>id<sub>e no ac</sub>ti<sub>ve po</sub>l<sub>-</sub> i<sub>cy</sub> <sub>gra</sub>di<sub>en</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

For trajectory $\tau _ { j } .$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>average</sub> l<sub>og-pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>biliti<sub>es</sub> <sub>over</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on-genera</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>un</sub>it<sub>s:</sub>

$$
\ell_ {j} (\theta) = \frac {1}{| \tau_ {j} |} \sum_ {m \in \tau_ {j}} \log \pi_ {\theta} (u _ {j, m} \mid x _ {j, m}),
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $x _ { j , m }$ <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>,</sub> <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ory,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ren-</sub> d<sub>ere</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e w</sub>h<sub>en ac</sub>ti<sub>on un</sub>it $u _ { j , m }$ <sup>i</sup>s <sub>g</sub>ener-<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t $B _ { \mathrm { a c t } } \subseteq B$ contain trajectories belonging to nond<sub>egenera</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>groups.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>on</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> <sub>group-re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>o</sub>b<sub>-</sub> jective (Shao et al. 2024) is

$$
\mathcal {L} _ {\text { policy }} = - \frac {1}{| \mathcal {B} _ {\text { act }} |} \sum_ {j \in \mathcal {B} _ {\text { act }}} A _ {j} \ell_ {j} (\theta) + \beta \mathcal {R} \left(\theta ; \pi_ {\text { anchor }}\right),
$$

where R denotes the frozen-<sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> re<sub>g</sub>ularizer when an-<sub>c</sub>h<sub>or</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>ena</sub>bl<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on–</sub>l<sub>anguage</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>one</sub> <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> frozen, and onl<sub>y</sub> the LoRA <sub>p</sub>arameters are o<sub>p</sub>timized (Hu et al. 2021). Restrictin<sub>g</sub> com<sub>p</sub>arisons to $G ( q , \kappa )$ <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>ue</sub> t<sub>o</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k difi<sub>cu</sub>lt<sub>y an</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>gu</sub>id<sub>ance w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> com<sub>p</sub>utin<sub>g</sub> relative advanta<sub>g</sub>es. The <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> bufer B stores only trajectories collected since the most recent LoRA update. The policy remains fixed while these trajectories are <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> O<sub>nce</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld i<sub>s</sub> <sub>reac</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>a</sub>t <sub>mos</sub>t <sub>one</sub> L<sub>o</sub>RA <sub>up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>emp</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>u</sub>f<sub>er</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>eare</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>regar</sub>d<sub>-</sub> l<sub>ess</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> it <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> <sub>an</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>compar</sub>i<sub>son</sub> <sub>group.</sub> N<sub>o</sub> trajectory is reused after the policy changes. Each trajectory <sub>re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ren</sub>d<sub>ere</sub>d<sub>-con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t id<sub>en</sub>tit<sub>y un</sub>d<sub>er w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h it <sub>was co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

<div class="mineru-algorithm" style="white-space: pre-wrap; font-family:monospace;">
Algorithm 1: CoAdapt-GUI test-time context–policy adaptation

Require: Source workflow library  $L_{src}$ , policy initialization  $\theta_{0}$ , adaptation tasks  $Q^{adapt}$ , frozen reflector, interaction budget B

Ensure: Frozen workflow state  $M^{\star}$  and policy parameters  $\theta^{\star}$ 

1: Retrieve the protocol-permitted source workflow context  $M_{0}$ 

2: Initialize target-grounded state  $M^{tgt} \leftarrow \varnothing$ 

3: Initialize policy  $\theta \leftarrow \theta_{0}$  and buffer  $B \leftarrow \varnothing$ 

4: while interaction budget remains do

5: Select a task  $q \in Q^{adapt}$ 

6: Select already materialized context variants for q

7: Collect matched rollouts D and record their task, context, and reset identities

8: Update the ratings of the evaluated variants using rewards in D

9: Propose and validate a child revision from traces in D

10: if the child passes all validation checks then

11: Add the child to the population for subsequent evaluation

12: end if

13: Add the same trajectories in D to B

14: if LoRA adaptation is enabled and B is ready then

15: Update LoRA from nondegenerate groups in B, if any

16: Clear B

17: end if

18: end while

19:  $M^{\star} \leftarrow$  highest-rated validated workflow state

20:  $\theta^{\star} \leftarrow \theta$ 

21: Freeze ( $M^{\star}, \theta^{\star}$ )

22: return ( $M^{\star}, \theta^{\star}$ )
</div>

## 4.3 New Task Templates in Unseen Apps

Setup. ## A AndroidWorld Plus Construction and Data Allocation

## A.1 Benchmark construction and executable filtering

AndroidWorld Plus is an ex<sub>p</sub>erimental extension of AndroidWorld (Rawles et al. 2025). We retain the 19 AndroidWorld a<sub>pp</sub>s <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>app-a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e execu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>dd <sub>s</sub>i<sub>x apps w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>su</sub>it<sub>es:</sub> C<sub>a</sub>l<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>or,</sub> S<sub>napsee</sub>d<sub>, an</sub>d Wiki<sub>pe</sub>di<sub>a</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> B MoCA (Lee et al. 2026), and Bluecoins, Ma<sub>p</sub>s.me, and Pi Music from AndroidLab (Xu et al. 2024). This <sub>p</sub>roduces 25 a<sub>pp</sub>s <sub>spann</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> 12 G<sub>oog</sub>l<sub>e</sub> Pl<sub>ay</sub> St<sub>ore ca</sub>t<sub>egor</sub>i<sub>es.</sub> W<sub>e use</sub> th<sub>e pu</sub>bli<sub>s</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d Pl<sub>ay</sub> St<sub>ore ca</sub>t<sub>egory as an ex</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l t<sub>axonomy,</sub> followin<sub>g</sub> Android Control (Li et al. 2024), rather than definin<sub>g</sub> cate<sub>g</sub>ories from task outcomes.

## A.3 Template and seed separation

Within each target app, we sort the executable template identifiers and create disjoint adaptation and evaluation sets. For an app with at least three templates, the first ⌈0.6n⌉ templates are assigned to adaptation and the remainder to evaluation. Two-template <sub>apps use a one–one sp</sub>lit<sub>; a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-</sub>t<sub>emp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e app con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>es on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> t<sub>o a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c ru</sub>l<sub>e pro</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> 60 <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>emp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es an</sub>d 35 <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>emp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es, w</sub>ith <sub>no</sub> t<sub>emp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e over</sub>l<sub>ap</sub> i<sub>n any</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>app.</sub> Th<sub>e sp</sub>lit <sub>was</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>ou</sub> <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d d<sub>oes no</sub>t <sub>use</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>rewar</sub>d<sub>s, po</sub>li<sub>cy pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons, or eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on ou</sub>t<sub>comes.</sub>

Each adaptation template is instantiated with seeds {30, 31, 32, 33, 34}, defining a 300-instance sampling pool. ## B.2 Instance seeds and leakage control

The released evaluation manifest instantiates every target template with seeds {7, 30, 1234}. Adaptation uses diferent seeds: Audio Recorder and Clock use {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9}; each OsmAnd template uses {1, 2, 3, 4}; each Tasks tem-<sub>p</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>es</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances; an</sub>d <sub>eac</sub>h B<sub>rocco</sub>li t<sub>emp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>es one</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance.</sub> Th<sub>e re</sub>l<sub>ease</sub>d T<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances use</sub> seeds {1, 2}, except TasksCompletedTasksForDate, which uses {2, 3}. Seven Broccoli templates use seed 1 and RecipeDeleteSingleWithRecipeWithNoise uses seed 2. Conse<sub>q</sub>uentl<sub>y</sub>, the intersection between ada<sub>p</sub>tation and evaluation (task\_id, seed) pairs is empty.

All <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>rev</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ons, po</sub>li<sub>cy up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es, an</sub>d <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on use on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> 40<sub>-</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on man</sub>if<sub>es</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e wor</sub>kfl<sub>ow</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>er are</sub> th<sub>en</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> th<sub>e</sub> 48<sub>-</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on man</sub>if<sub>es</sub>t i<sub>s opene</sub>d<sub>.</sub> N<sub>o</sub> h<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>-ou</sub>t t<sub>as</sub>k i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance,</sub> trajectory, reward, or outcome is used for adaptation or model selection.

<table><tr><td>Stage</td><td>Contract</td><td>Failure handling</td></tr><tr><td>Source synthesis</td><td>The response must parse as a JSON object containing the typed app-bound and transferable workflow fields. Model-authored metadata are discarded; the caller records the evidence count, synthesis model and settings, and a hash of the complete prompt.</td><td>A malformed response or a missing required component is rejected rather than converted into a source workflow artifact.</td></tr><tr><td>Source consolidation</td><td>Entries are merged only within an externally defined app category. The result must contain at least one typed transferable entry. The hard source-library linter checks the union of contributing source apps for app-name variants, package/resource identifiers, and app-specific state identifiers.</td><td>An empty, malformed, or lint-failing library is not written and therefore cannot be retrieved for a target app.</td></tr><tr><td>Target reflection</td><td>Only adaptation-task goals, reset identities, compact traces, and executable task rewards are supplied. The prompt requires every suggested change to cite adaptation-trace evidence and remain app-agnostic.</td><td>Reflection text cannot directly modify the workflow state; it is used only as evidence for the subsequent typed-diff request.</td></tr><tr><td>Typed revision</td><td>The response must parse into typed add, modify, or remove operations. In target adaptation, only layer2/category operations are retained, and the applied diff must change the parent state.</td><td>Malformed JSON is retried up to three times. Empty diffs, all-skipped operations, app-bound operations, and unchanged children are rejected.</td></tr><tr><td>Candidate admission</td><td>The caller records the parent, task, iteration, and revision summary. A valid child enters the population without inheriting the rewards of the rollouts that produced it.</td><td>The candidate affects final context selection only after it is sampled and rated using subsequent matched target rollouts.</td></tr><tr><td>Frozen evaluation</td><td>The selected context and LoRA adapter are loaded as immutable artifacts, and only the held-out manifest is opened.</td><td>The reflector, population controller, and optimizer are disabled; evaluation trajectories and rewards cannot alter either adapted state.</td></tr></table>
